````
you wrote it, on this run's storage volume, at the path it already has — it is
not moved, renamed or copied. A later round reads it there, by that absolute
workspace path, so a checkpoint you keep is a checkpoint the next round can

your cwd to GitHub but skips every file of 100 MB or

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-5/evaluation-4/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)
````

```
import json
d=json.load(open('$A/preview_eval_out.json'))
def show(x,depth=0,maxd=4,key=''):
    pad='  '*depth
    if isinstance(x,dict):
        print(pad+key+'{dict %d keys}'%len(x))
        if depth<maxd:
            for k,v in list(x.items())[:40]:
                show(v,depth+1,maxd,k+': ')
    elif isinstance(x,list):
        print(pad+key+'[list %d]'%len(x))
        if depth<maxd and x:
            show(x[0],depth+1,maxd,'[0]: ')
    else:
        s=repr(x)
        print(pad+key+s[:150])
show(d,0,5)
```

### [53] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 11:33:03 UTC

```
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_5/art_mgek9PfVBNbh/make_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json, the input bundle of code_demo.ipynb.

The notebook runs the record-correction stage of eval.py (artifact art_mgek9PfVBNbh): sections (0a)-(0h) and the
iteration-1 base compositions.  That stage opens 53 files of eight dependency artifacts (about 100 MB, mostly
generated programs and per-sample metadata).  This script packs those 53 files into ONE dataset, `eval_inputs`, with
one example per file, keeping only what eval.py reads:

- result files (.json) are copied whole, except exp20 `results/cells.json`, which is cut to the six `<task>|S3|4`
  cells eval.py reads;
- row files (.jsonl) keep the rows eval.py selects and the fields it reads (see ROWS), in their original order, stored
  column-wise (`columns` plus one value list per row).

Every example records the sha256 and size of the original file.  The script also checks that eval.py's substring
filters (`must_contain`) select the same rows from the trimmed lines as from the original lines.

Dependency folders are found as in the artifact's src/io_util.py: <AII_DEPS_ROOT>/<artifact_id>, or
AII_DEP_<artifact_id> for a single one (non-alphanumeric characters of the id replaced by '_').

Run:  AII_DEPS_ROOT=<folder holding the eight dependency artifacts> python3 make_mini_demo_data.py
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
DEPS_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("AII_DEPS_ROOT", str(HERE.parent)))
DEP_IDS = {
    "exp23": "art_k6nzFCDLOjQP",  # held-out family (gemma-3-4b-it), iteration 4
    "exp20": "art_Cx9JOh8soBw7",  # site experiment, iteration 4
    "exp22": "art_SHaWv77P1XCI",  # plan subspace / escape fractions, iteration 4
    "exp19": "art_mWn-MQEnNelg",  # plan noise knob table, iteration 4
    "exp21": "art_Yu6GN6pLxW8f",  # same-seed temperature vs plan noise, iteration 4
    "exp5": "art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy",   # iteration-1 base-model data
    "bench": "art_0ndRfWKBWclR",  # iteration-2 coding bench
}
BENCH_TASKS = ["R1_non_overlapping_intervals", "R2_cycle_detection", "R3_topological_order", "R6_first_last_position",
               "T03_kth_smallest", "T04_substring_search", "T05_two_sum", "T06_find_duplicate", "T08_fibonacci",
               "T10_connected_components", "T11_longest_increasing_subsequence", "T13_merge_intervals",
               "T14_top_k_frequent"]
# every file eval.py's corrections stage opens, in the order it first opens them
FILES = [("bench", f"bench/{d}/spec.json") for d in BENCH_TASKS] + [
    ("exp23", "data/samples.jsonl"), ("exp23", "predictions/commit_preban.json"), ("exp23", "results/c3a.json"),
    ("exp23", "results/bans.json"), ("exp23", "results/pilot.json"), ("exp23", "results/c1_prices.json"),
    ("exp23", "results/posthoc_t2_relabel.json"), ("exp23", "results/c3b.json"),
    ("exp20", "data/samples.jsonl"), ("exp20", "results/c1_site.json"), ("exp20", "results/paired_flips.json"),
    ("exp20", "results/cells.json"), ("exp20", "results/transmission.json"), ("exp20", "results/rederive_headlines.json"),
    ("exp22", "results/c3b.json"), ("exp22", "results/escape.json"), ("exp22", "results/screen.json"),
    ("exp22", "inputs/exp13/results/c1_events.jsonl"), ("exp22", "inputs/exp16/results/c1_events.jsonl"),
    ("exp22", "data/labels.jsonl"),
    ("exp19", "data/samples.jsonl"), ("exp19", "results/plain_vs_twin.json"), ("exp19", "results/deviations.jsonl"),
    ("exp19", "results/prices.json"),
    ("exp22", "results/units.json"), ("exp20", "results/units_summary.json"), ("exp22", "results/deviations.json"),
    ("exp23", "results/qwen_ratio.json"), ("exp21", "results/unit_sensitivity.json"), ("exp21", "results/gpu_hours.json"),
    ("exp21", "results/c1seed.json"), ("exp22", "results/rederive_extra.json"), ("exp22", "results/explore.json"),
    ("exp20", "results/boundary.json"), ("exp19", "results/knob_rows.jsonl"),
    ("exp21", "results/vllm_engine.json"), ("exp21", "results/thinking.json"), ("exp21", "data/raw_thinking.jsonl"),
    ("exp5", "runs/labelled_base_chat__baseline.jsonl"), ("exp5", "runs/labelled_base_tf__baseline.jsonl"),
]
EXP20_ARMS = {"TWIN", "S1E_a1", "S2E_a1"} | {f"S{s}_a{a}" for s in range(6) for a in (1, 2, 4)}
# row files: (fields eval.py reads, rows eval.py uses); a file not listed here keeps every row and field
ROWS = {
    # (0a) ban rows, twin rows, and the shortest-path task's plan-noise and novelty rows
    ("exp23", "data/samples.jsonl"): (
        {"task", "stage", "arm", "label", "passed"},
        lambda x: (x["stage"], x["arm"]) in (("BAN", "ban"), ("A", "twin"))
        or (x["task"] == "T2" and x["arm"] in ("pn_a1", "pn_a2", "novelty"))),
    # (0b) twin rows, the alpha 1/2/4 arms of sites S0-S5, the energy-matched S1E/S2E arms and every S3 row; the
    # program text is kept because eval.py re-applies exp20's rule D13 (no function named solve -> invalid)
    ("exp20", "data/samples.jsonl"): (
        {"task", "slot", "arm", "site", "label", "passed", "code"},
        lambda x: x["arm"] in EXP20_ARMS or x["site"] == "S3"),
    # (0c)/(0e) the POP_d direction rows and the twin rows (read with must_contain '"POP_d' and '"TWIN"')
    ("exp22", "data/labels.jsonl"): (
        {"task", "arm", "label", "passed"},
        lambda x: x["arm"].startswith("POP_d") or x["arm"] == "TWIN"),
    # (0d) PNPLAIN_a1 and PN_a1 rows, twin rows, and the grid task's PLAIN rows
    ("exp19", "data/samples.jsonl"): (
        {"task", "arm", "crn_key", "label", "passed"},
        lambda x: x["arm"] in ("PNPLAIN_a1", "PN_a1", "TWIN") or (x["arm"] == "PLAIN" and x["task"] == "T1")),
    # (0h) decoding settings of the thinking-on arm
    ("exp21", "data/raw_thinking.jsonl"): ({"temperature", "top_k"}, None),
    # iteration-1 base compositions
    ("exp5", "runs/labelled_base_chat__baseline.jsonl"): ({"task", "label", "passed"}, None),
    ("exp5", "runs/labelled_base_tf__baseline.jsonl"): ({"task", "label", "passed"}, None),
}
MUST_CONTAIN = {("exp22", "data/labels.jsonl"): ('"POP_d', '"TWIN"'),
                ("exp19", "data/samples.jsonl"): ('"PN', '"TWIN"', '"PLAIN"')}
JSON_TRIM = {("exp20", "results/cells.json"): lambda d: {k: v for k, v in d.items() if k.endswith("|S3|4")}}


def dep_dir(art_id: str) -> Path:
    return Path(os.environ.get("AII_DEP_" + re.sub(r"\W", "_", art_id), str(DEPS_ROOT / art_id)))


def sha256_file(p: Path) -> str:
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with p.open("rb") as fh:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(1 << 20), b""):
            h.update(chunk)
    return h.hexdigest()


def pack_jsonl(dep: str, rel: str, p: Path) -> dict:
    fields, keep = ROWS.get((dep, rel), (None, None))
    tokens = MUST_CONTAIN.get((dep, rel), ())
    columns: list[str] | None = None
    rows, n = [], 0
    with p.open() as fh:
        for ln in fh:
            if not ln.strip():
                continue
            n += 1
            x = json.loads(ln)
            if keep is not None and not keep(x):
                continue
            y = {k: v for k, v in x.items() if fields is None or k in fields}
            if columns is None:
                columns = list(y)
            assert list(y) == columns, f"{dep}:{rel} row {n} has keys {list(y)}, expected {columns}"
            for tok in tokens:  # eval.py's substring filter must pick the same rows from the trimmed line
                assert (tok in ln) == (tok in json.dumps(y)), f"{dep}:{rel} row {n}: must_contain {tok} changes"
            rows.append([y[k] for k in columns])
    return {"format": "jsonl", "n_rows_original": n, "n_rows_kept": len(rows),
            "fields_kept": "all" if fields is None else columns, "columns": columns, "rows": rows}


def main() -> None:
    examples = []
    for dep, rel in FILES:
        art = DEP_IDS[dep]
        p = dep_dir(art) / rel
        ex = {"source": f"{art}:{rel}", "dep": dep, "artifact_id": art, "path": rel,
              "original_sha256": sha256_file(p), "original_bytes": p.stat().st_size}
        if rel.endswith(".jsonl"):
            ex.update(pack_jsonl(dep, rel, p))
        else:
            content = json.loads(p.read_text())
            trim = JSON_TRIM.get((dep, rel))
            ex.update({"format": "json", "trimmed": trim is not None, "content": trim(content) if trim else content})
        examples.append(ex)
        kept = f"{ex['n_rows_kept']} of {ex['n_rows_original']} rows" if ex["format"] == "jsonl" else "whole"
        print(f"{ex['source']:60s} {kept}")
    out = {
        "metadata": {
            "title": "Record fixes and frozen rival predictors: inputs of the record-correction stage",
            "description": "The 53 dependency files that eval.py's corrections stage (sections 0a-0h and the "
                           "iteration-1 base compositions) opens, one example per file, trimmed to the rows and "
                           "fields eval.py reads. Row files are stored column-wise. original_sha256 / original_bytes "
                           "identify the untrimmed file.",
            "source_script": "eval.py of artifact art_mgek9PfVBNbh",
            "dependencies": DEP_IDS,
            "built_by": "make_mini_demo_data.py",
        },
        "datasets": [{"dataset": "eval_inputs", "examples": examples}],
    }
    (HERE / "mini_demo_data.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, separators=(",", ":")))
    print(f"wrote mini_demo_data.json: {len(examples)} examples, "
          f"{(HERE / 'mini_demo_data.json').stat().st_size / 1e6:.2f} MB")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

<< 20), b\"\"):\n            h.update(chunk)\n    return h.hexdigest()\n\n\ndef pack_jsonl(dep: str, rel: str, p: Path) -> dict:\n    fields, keep = ROWS.get((dep, rel), (None, None))\n    tokens = MUST_CONTAIN.get((dep, rel), ())\n    columns: list[str] | None = None\n    rows, n = [], 0\n    with p.open() as fh:\n        for ln in fh:\n            if not ln.strip():\n                continue\n            n += 1\n            x = json.loads(ln)\n            if keep is not None and not keep(x):\n                continue\n            y = {k: v for k, v in x.items() if fields is None or k in fields}\n            if columns is None:\n                columns = list(y)\n            assert list(y) == columns, f\"{dep}:{rel} row {n} has keys {list(y)}, expected {columns}\"\n            for tok in tokens:  # eval.py's substring filter must pick the same rows from the trimmed line\n                assert (tok in ln) == (tok in json.dumps(y)), f\"{dep}:{rel} row {n}: must_contain {tok} changes\"\n            rows.append([y[k] for k in columns])\n    return {\"format\": \"jsonl\", \"n_rows_original\": n, \"n_rows_kept\": len(rows),\n            \"fields_kept\": \"all\" if fields is None else columns, \"columns\": columns, \"rows\": rows}\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    examples = []\n    for dep, rel in FILES:\n        art = DEP_IDS[dep]\n        p = dep_dir(art) / rel\n        ex = {\"source\": f\"{art}:{rel}\", \"dep\": dep, \"artifact_id\": art, \"path\": rel,\n              \"original_sha256\": sha256_file(p), \"original_bytes\": p.stat().st_size}\n        if rel.endswith(\".jsonl\"):\n            ex.update(pack_jsonl(dep, rel, p))\n        else:\n            content = json.loads(p.read_text())\n            trim = JSON_TRIM.get((dep, rel))\n            ex.update({\"format\": \"json\", \"trimmed\": trim is not None, \"content\": trim(content) if trim else content})\n        examples.append(ex)\n        kept = f\"{ex['n_rows_kept']} of     """1-indexed, a inclusive, b exclusive; trailing blank lines dropped."""
```
    s = ev[a - 1:b - 1]
    while s and not s[-1].strip():
        s.pop()
    return "".join(s)

    return text.replace(old, new)

```
```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_5/art_mgek9PfVBNbh/code_demo.ipynb', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'markdown', 'new_source': '# Record fixes and frozen rival predictors: demo of `eval.py`\n\n**Artifact `art_mgek9PfVBNbh`** · iteration 5, Step 0 · CPU only, no model is run.\n\n**Research context.** The project asks how "creativity" shows up inside an LLM. Asked to write a path-search function, does the model always produce BFS? Can a prompt ban, a plan-noise push along a random activation-space direction, a seed or a temperature move it to another algorithm? Earlier iterations ran Qwen3-8B (and a held-out Gemma-3-4B family) on 15 coding tasks. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, using runtime traces, AST rules and a judge.\n\n**What this artifact does.** `eval.py` re-derives every number that the iteration-4 report states about those runs from the raw rows of eight dependency artifacts. It writes each fix as an exact `old_text -> new_text` replacement: 35 entries, each marked `iteration-5 correction`. Every value carries a provenance tag: RE-DERIVED, READ, CARRIED or NOT_COMPUTABLE. When the record states a value, the value also gets a status against the record\'s tolerance:\n- counts must match exactly;\n- rates must agree within 0.005;\n- correlations and log scores within 0.01;\n- intervals within 0.02.\n\n| section | what is re-derived |\n|---|---|\n| (0a) | held-out Gemma verdict: does push movability `E_t` predict which bans the model obeys? Balanced accuracy, Fisher exact test and Spearman, for each compliance measure |\n| (0b) | where a push acts: which prefill positions change the algorithm (sites S0–S5, energy-matched S1E/S2E, paired flips with McNemar) |\n| (0c) | landing forecasts on matched events: log scores of the push forecast vs association vs proximity |\n| (0d) | executed but unreported results: PNPLAIN, hull deviation, Llama block, dose units, GPU hours |\n| (0e) | per-direction distributions instead of best cells; split-half; signed progress; Table 147 depths |\n| (0g), (0h) | exposure-class rule; Table 76 cross-reference (decoding of the thinking-on arm) |\n| base | iteration-1 base-model compositions |\n\n**What this notebook runs.** The record-correction stage of `eval.py` (`corrections()`). The code is copied cell by cell from `eval.py` and its helpers `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`; the few notebook changes are marked `# notebook:`. In the original, `main()` then launches four separate scripts: the rival freeze, the rival scoring, a live quote check and a stdlib audit. It then assembles `eval_out.json`. Those scripts need further inputs and network access, so the notebook describes them but does not run them.\n\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds one dataset, `eval_inputs`, with 53 examples: one per dependency file that the stage opens. The files are trimmed to the rows and fields `eval.py` reads, which cuts about 100 MB of inputs down to 3.3 MB. With the original Monte Carlo sizes, the stage reproduces the artifact\'s published `corrections.json`, `results/corrections_metrics.json` and its 14 tables byte for byte on this bundle.'}
```

"updated_file": "{\n \"cells\": [\n  {\n   \"cell_type\": \"markdown\",\n   \"id\": \"ee66aab8\",\n   \"source\": \"# Record fixes and frozen rival predictors: demo of `eval.py`\\n\\n**Artifact `art_mgek9PfVBNbh`** · iteration 5, Step 0 · CPU only, no model is run.\\n\\n**Research context.** The project asks how \\\"creativity\\\" shows up inside an LLM. Asked to write a path-search function, does the model always produce BFS? Can a prompt ban, a plan-noise push along a random activation-space direction, a seed or a temperature move it to another algorithm? Earlier iterations ran Qwen3-8B (and a held-out Gemma-3-4B family) on 15 coding tasks. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, using runtime traces, AST rules and a judge.\\n\\n**What this artifact does.** `eval.py` re-derives every number that the iteration-4 report states about those runs from the raw rows of eight dependency artifacts. It writes each fix as an exact `old_text -> new_text` replacement: 35 entries, each marked `iteration-5 correction`. Every value carries a provenance tag: RE-DERIVED, READ, CARRIED or NOT_COMPUTABLE. When the record states a value, the value also gets a status against the record's tolerance:\\n- counts must match exactly;\\n- rates must agree within 0.005;\\n- correlations and log scores within 0.01;\\n- intervals within 0.02.\\n\\n| section | what is re-derived |\\n|---|---|\\n| (0a) | held-out Gemma verdict: does push movability `E_t` predict which bans the model obeys? Balanced accuracy, Fisher exact test and Spearman, for each compliance measure |\\n| (0b) | where a push acts: which prefill positions change the algorithm (sites S0–S5, energy-matched S1E/S2E, paired flips with McNemar) |\\n| (0c) | landing forecasts on matched events: log scores of the push forecast vs association vs proximity |\\n| (0d) | executed but unreported results: PNPLAIN, hull deviation, Llama block, dose units, GPU hours |\\n| (0e) | per-direction distributions instead of best cells; split-half; signed progress; Table 147 depths |\\n| (0g), (0h) | exposure-class rule; Table 76 cross-reference (decoding of the thinking-on arm) |\\n| base | iteration-1 base-model compositions |\\n\\n**What this notebook runs.** The record-correction stage of `eval.py` (`corrections()`). The code is copied cell by cell from `eval.py` and its helpers `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`; the few notebook changes are marked `# notebook:`. In the original, `main()` then launches four separate scripts: the rival freeze, the rival scoring, a live quote check and a stdlib audit. It then assembles `eval_out.json`. Those scripts need further inputs and network access, so the notebook describes them but does not run them.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds one dataset, `eval_inputs`, with 53 examples: one per dependency file that the stage opens. The files are trimmed to the rows and fields `eval.py` reads, which cuts about 100 MB of inputs down to 3.3 MB. Balanced accuracy, Fisher exact test and Spearman, for each compliance measure |\\n| (0b) | where a push acts: which prefill positions change the algorithm (sites S0–S5, energy-matched S1E/S2E, paired flips with McNemar) |\\n| (0c) | landing forecasts on matched events: log scores of the push forecast vs association vs proximity |\\n| (0d) | executed but unreported results: PNPLAIN, hull deviation, Llama block, dose units, GPU hours |\\n| (0e) | per-direction distributions instead of best cells; split-half; signed progress; Table 147 depths |\\n| (0g), (0h) | exposure-class rule; Table 76 cross-reference (decoding of the thinking-on arm) |\\n| base | iteration-1 base-model compositions |\\n\\n**What this notebook runs.** The record-correction stage of `eval.py` (`corrections()`). The code is copied cell by cell from `eval.py` and its helpers `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`; the few notebook changes are marked `# notebook:`. In the original, `main()` then launches four separate scripts: the rival freeze, the rival scoring, a live quote check and a stdlib audit. It then assembles `eval_out.json`. Those scripts need further inputs and network access, so the notebook describes them but does not run them.\\n\\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds one dataset, `eval_inputs`, with 53 examples: one per dependency file that the stage opens. The files are trimmed to the rows and fields `eval.py` reads, which cuts about 100 MB of inputs down to 3.3 MB. With the original Monte Carlo sizes, the stage reproduces the artifact's published `corrections.json`, `results/corrections_metrics.json` and its 14 tables byte for byte on this bundle.\",\n Asked to write a path-search function, does the model always produce BFS? Can a prompt ban, a plan-noise push along a random activation-space direction, a seed or a temperature move it to another algorithm? Earlier iterations ran Qwen3-8B (and a held-out Gemma-3-4B family) on 15 coding tasks. Every generated program was labelled with the algorithm it implements, using runtime traces, AST rules and a judge.\n\n**What this artifact does.** `eval.py` re-derives every number that the iteration-4 report states about those runs from the raw rows of eight dependency artifacts. It writes each fix as an exact `old_text -> new_text` replacement: 35 entries, each marked `iteration-5 correction`. Every value carries a provenance tag: RE-DERIVED, READ, CARRIED or NOT_COMPUTABLE. When the record states a value, the value also gets a status against the record\'s tolerance:\n- counts must match exactly;\n- rates must agree within 0.005;\n- correlations and log scores within 0.01;\n- intervals within 0.02.\n\n| section | what is re-derived |\n|---|---|\n| (0a) | held-out Gemma verdict: does push movability `E_t` predict which bans the model obeys? Balanced accuracy, Fisher exact test and Spearman, for each compliance measure |\n| (0b) | where a push acts: which prefill positions change the algorithm (sites S0–S5, energy-matched S1E/S2E, paired flips with McNemar) |\n| (0c) | landing forecasts on matched events: log scores of the push forecast vs association vs proximity |\n| (0d) | executed but unreported results: PNPLAIN, hull deviation, Llama block, dose units, GPU hours |\n| (0e) | per-direction distributions instead of best cells; split-half; signed progress; Table 147 depths |\n| (0g), (0h) | exposure-class rule; Table 76 cross-reference (decoding of the thinking-on arm) |\n| base | iteration-1 base-model compositions |\n\n**What this notebook runs.** The record-correction stage of `eval.py` (`corrections()`). The code is copied cell by cell from `eval.py` and its helpers `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`; the few notebook changes are marked `# notebook:`. In the original, `main()` then launches four separate scripts: the rival freeze, the rival scoring, a live quote check and a stdlib audit. It then assembles `eval_out.json`. Those scripts need further inputs and network access, so the notebook describes them but does not run them.\n\n**Data.** `mini_demo_data.json` holds one dataset, `eval_inputs`, with 53 examples. Each example is one file that the stage opens, from 7 of the 8 dependency artifacts. The files are trimmed to the rows and fields `eval.py` reads, which cuts 77.7 MB of inputs down to 3.3 MB. With the original Monte Carlo sizes, the stage reproduces the artifact\'s published `corrections.json`, `results/corrections_metrics.json` and its 14 tables byte for byte on this bundle.'}
```

Each of the 15 tasks gets three quantities.\n- **E_t** (movability): the share of plan-push directions that produced a confirmed escape from the default algorithm. A task with `E_t > 0` is predicted to comply with the ban.\n- **A_t** (catalogue-only avoidance): the share of ban-arm programs that implement another *known* algorithm of the task's catalogue. A task with `A_t ≥ 0.5` complies.\n- **default-share drop** (sensitivity): the twin's default share minus the ban arm's default share.\n\n`c3a_block()` scores the prediction with balanced accuracy (with a bootstrap CI), Fisher's exact test, Spearman ρ with a one-sided permutation p, and Kendall τ-b. It then applies the frozen verdict rule:\n- **CONFIRMED** if BA ≥ 0.8, Fisher p < 0.05 and ρ ≥ 0.5;\n- **DISCONFIRMED** if BA ≤ 0.6;\n- **INCONCLUSIVE** otherwise.\n\n`sec_0a()` evaluates the primary measure, the sensitivity and several descriptive subsets. It also does the following:\n- **Default rule.** It applies the iteration-5 rule: a task has a DEFAULT only if its twin has at least 50 programs and its modal *correct* algorithm has a share of at least 0.6. The grid task's twin is tied at BFS 25 / DFS 25, so grid is NO DEFAULT.\n- **Placebo.** It permutes `E_t` across tasks; the mean BA should come out near 0.5.\n- **Further checks.** It checks the labeller caveat on the shortest-path task and the Gemma landing contest.\n- **Outputs.** It writes Tables 159, 160 and 163 and their corrections."}
```

```
"""Exact, asserted replacements inside one notebook cell (by id).  usage: nb_edit.py NB EDITS.json
EDITS.json: [{"cell": id, "old": str, "new": str}, ...]; each old must occur exactly once in that cell."""
import json, sys
nb_path, edits_path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
nb = json.load(open(nb_path))
by = {c.get("id"): c for c in nb["cells"]}
for ed in json.load(open(edits_path)):
    src = "".join(by[ed["cell"]]["source"])
    assert src.count(ed["old"]) == 1, (ed["cell"], src.count(ed["old"]), ed["old"][:80])
    by[ed["cell"]]["source"] = src.replace(ed["old"], ed["new"]).splitlines(keepends=True)
open(nb_path, "w").write(json.dumps(nb, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
print("applied", len(json.load(open(edits_path))), "edits")
EOF

edits = [
 {"cell": "a739b6c0", "old": 'pt = METRICS["0a"]["per_task"]\n', "new": 'import textwrap\n\npt = METRICS["0a"]["per_task"]\n'},

  "old": '''    for t, row in pt.items():
        e, y = row["E_t"], row[key]

        ax.annotate(NAMES[t], (e, y), xytext=(5, 3), textcoords="offset points", fontsize=7)
''',
  "new": '''    spots = defaultdict(list)  # tasks at the same coordinates share one label
    for t, row in pt.items():
        e, y = row["E_t"], row[key]
        col = "tab:orange" if e > 0 else "tab:gray"
        ax.scatter(e, y, s=80, facecolors=col if y >= 0.5 else "none", edgecolors=col, linewidths=1.8, zorder=3)
        spots[(round(e, 3), round(y, 3))].append(t)
    quiet = []  # E_t = 0 and below 0.5 (correctly predicted not to comply): listed in a box, not labelled one by one
    for (e, y), ts in spots.items():
        if e == 0 and y < 0.5:
            quiet += ts
        else:
            ax.annotate(", ".join(NAMES[t] for t in ts), (e, y), xytext=(5, 3), textcoords="offset points", fontsize=7)
    ax.text(0.12, 0.03, textwrap.fill(f"$E_t$ = 0 and below 0.5 ({len(quiet)} tasks): " +
                                      ", ".join(NAMES[t] for t in quiet), 62),
            transform=ax.transAxes, fontsize=7, va="bottom", bbox=dict(boxstyle="round", fc="w", ec="0.7"))
'''},
 {"cell": "a739b6c0", "old": 'axs[0, 0].legend(fontsize=7, loc="lower right", title="complies = measure >= 0.5", title_fontsize=7)',
```

### [201] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 11:54:26 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_5/art_mgek9PfVBNbh/README.md

# Demo notebook: record fixes and frozen rival predictors

This folder holds a runnable demo of `eval.py` from artifact `art_mgek9PfVBNbh`, iteration 5, Step 0. That step is CPU only and runs no model. It re-derives every number the iteration-4 report states about the earlier coding-algorithm experiments from the raw rows of their artifacts. Each fix is written as an exact `old_text -> new_text` replacement.

The research question behind those experiments: when Qwen3-8B (or a held-out Gemma-3-4B) writes a solution to a coding task, it usually picks one default algorithm, such as BFS for a path search. Does a prompt ban, a plan-noise push in activation space, a seed or a temperature move it to a different one?

## What the notebook does

`code_demo.ipynb` runs the record-correction stage of `eval.py` (`corrections()`). That is sections (0a)–(0h) and the iteration-1 base compositions:

- **(0a) Held-out Gemma verdict.** Does push movability `E_t` predict ban compliance? Balanced accuracy, Fisher exact test and Spearman, for each compliance measure, plus a placebo.
- **(0b) Site of the push.** Which prefill positions change the algorithm (S0–S5, the energy-matched S1E/S2E), and paired flips with an exact McNemar test.
- **(0c) Landing forecasts.** Push, association and proximity forecasts scored by log score on matched event sets.
- **(0d) Executed but unreported results.** PNPLAIN, the hull deviation, the Llama block (CARRIED), dose units and GPU hours.
- **(0e) Per-direction distributions.** All 624 (task, α, direction) cells instead of the best cells; split-half; signed progress; Table 147 depths.
- **(0g)/(0h) Exposure-class rule and Table 76 cross-reference.**

The stage produces 35 corrections. Each value carries a provenance tag and a status against the record: 186 MATCH, 1 CORRECTED, 15 NEW and 4 CARRIED. The CORRECTED value is signed progress: the record's "22 of 35" cells is 22 of 24.

The code is copied cell by cell from `eval.py`, `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`. Every notebook change is marked `# notebook:` in the code:

- the script folder `WS` becomes `demo_outputs/`;
- dependencies default to the unpacked bundle (`DEPS_ROOT`);
- `import stats_util as su` becomes a namespace `su` over the inlined functions;
- the Monte Carlo sizes `N_BOOT`, `N_PERM` and the (0a) placebo count come from a config cell;
- the run cell clears `CORR`/`METRICS`, so re-running it does not duplicate entries.

`run_script()` and `main()` are reproduced verbatim in a markdown cell but not executed. In the original they launch four more scripts, each needing inputs that are not in this bundle:

- `rivals/compute_rivals.py` freezes the eight rival predictors R1–R8 and K;
- `rivals/score_rivals.py` scores them;
- `quotes/verify_quotes.py` checks quotes live and needs network access;
- `audit/rederive.py` is the independent stdlib audit.

After those scripts, `main()` assembles `eval_out.json`.

## Results it reproduces

With the original Monte Carlo sizes (10,000 bootstrap replicates, 200,000 permutations, 1,000 placebo draws), the notebook's outputs are byte-identical to the published artifact's: `corrections.json`, `results/corrections_metrics.json`, `ids.json` and all 14 tables. All 23 headline values it prints equal the published `metrics_agg` values, including:

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| (0a) primary, catalogue-only avoidance | BA 0.739 [0.42, 0.95], Fisher p 0.235, Spearman 0.537: **INCONCLUSIVE** |
| (0a) sensitivity, default-share drop | BA 0.568, Fisher p 1.0, Spearman 0.391: **DISCONFIRMED** |
| (0a) without grid path and shortest path | BA 0.614, Spearman 0.313, one-sided permutation p 0.144 |
| (0b) read position / condition sentence / late blocks | 0 of 526, 0 of 527, 6 of 521 correct programs off the default |
| (0b) energy-matched read position, excess over twin | +0.231 (find duplicate), +0.179 (connected components) |
| (0b) generated-token noise vs plan site, find duplicate, α 2 | 12:0 paired flips, exact McNemar p 0.000488 |
| (0c) log score on the 3 push-defined events | push −0.761, association −3.115, proximity −2.918 |
| (0c) log score over all 5 events, uniform fill | push −1.366, association −3.462 |
| dose check (ban unit / residual norm vs E_pool) | Spearman −0.059, AUC 0.489 |

The notebook ends with a six-panel figure:

- **(a), (b)** `E_t` against each compliance measure, one point per Gemma task;
- **(c)** balanced accuracy of every (0a) variant against the frozen verdict thresholds;
- **(d)** off-default share by site;
- **(e)** log scores per forecast family;
- **(f)** the per-direction off-default shares of all 624 cells.

## Layout

| path | what it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | the demo notebook, saved with the outputs of a full run |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | the input bundle (3.3 MB): one dataset, `eval_inputs`, with 53 examples, one per dependency file the corrections stage opens, trimmed to what `eval.py` reads |
| `make_mini_demo_data.py` | rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the dependency artifacts |
| `README.md` | this file |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | storage decisions (none needed; see below) |

Running the notebook creates `demo_outputs/`, which is not kept in this folder:

- `corrections.json`, `ids.json`;
- `results/`, `tables/`, `logs/eval.log`;
- `summary.png`;
- `deps/`, the unpacked bundle.

## How to run

**Colab.** Open `code_demo.ipynb` and choose *Run all*. The data is loaded from the repository's raw GitHub URL. The install cell adds only `loguru==0.7.3` and uses Colab's own numpy, scipy and matplotlib.

**Locally** (Python 3.12):

```bash
python3.12 -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/pip install jupyter ipykernel
.venv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

When `google.colab` is absent, the install cell installs Colab's versions: `numpy==2.0.2`, `scipy==1.16.3`, `matplotlib==3.10.0`. If the GitHub URL is unreachable, the notebook falls back to the local `mini_demo_data.json`.

Measured runtimes in a fresh environment (the whole notebook includes package installs from a warm cache):

| `N_BOOT` / `N_PERM` / `N_PLACEBO` | corrections stage | whole notebook |
|---|---|---|
| 10 / 100 / 10 | 1.6 s | 15 s |
| 1,000 / 20,000 / 100 | 2.6 s | 16 s |
| 10,000 / 200,000 / 1,000 (original, used) | 11.1 s | 24 s |

Point estimates do not depend on these sizes. Only bootstrap intervals, permutation p-values and the placebo mean do.

## The input bundle

The corrections stage opens 53 files from 7 of the artifact's 8 dependencies, 77.7 MB in total:

| short name | artifact |
|---|---|
| exp23, held-out Gemma family | `art_k6nzFCDLOjQP` |
| exp20, site experiment | `art_Cx9JOh8soBw7` |
| exp22, plan subspace and escape fractions | `art_SHaWv77P1XCI` |
| exp19, plan-noise knob table | `art_mWn-MQEnNelg` |
| exp21, temperature vs plan noise | `art_Yu6GN6pLxW8f` |
| exp5, iteration-1 base-model data | `art_HF8nK8B_Lqyy` |
| coding bench | `art_0ndRfWKBWclR` |

`make_mini_demo_data.py` keeps:

- **result files (`.json`)** whole, except exp20 `results/cells.json`, which is cut to the six `<task>|S3|4` cells `eval.py` reads;
- **row files (`.jsonl`)** with only the rows `eval.py` selects and the fields it reads, in their original order, stored column-wise:
  - exp23 samples: the ban, twin and shortest-path plan-noise/novelty rows; 2,400 of 10,730.
  - exp20 samples: twin rows, the α 1/2/4 arms of S0–S5, S1E/S2E and every S3 row, with the program text that rule D13 parses; 3,968 of 4,752.
  - exp22 labels: the `POP_d` direction rows and the twin rows; 10,520 of 13,500.
  - exp19 samples: the `PNPLAIN_a1`, `PN_a1`, twin and grid-task `PLAIN` rows; 1,900 of 10,100.
  - Smaller row files keep every row.

Each example records the original file's sha256 and size.

The bundle was checked in three ways:

1. The builder asserts that `eval.py`'s substring filters (`must_contain`) pick the same rows from the trimmed lines as from the original ones.
2. Running the unmodified `eval.py` `corrections()` on the unpacked bundle and on the original files gave byte-identical `corrections.json`, `results/corrections_metrics.json`, `ids.json` and 14 tables. Both runs also matched the published artifact's files.
3. The notebook's own run reproduces the same 17 files byte for byte.

To rebuild the bundle, point the builder at the dependency artifacts. They are resolved as in the artifact's `src/io_util.py`: `<AII_DEPS_ROOT>/<artifact_id>`, and `AII_DEP_<artifact_id>` overrides a single one (non-alphanumeric characters of the id replaced by `_`):

```bash
AII_DEPS_ROOT=<folder holding the dependency artifacts> python3 make_mini_demo_data.py
```

To run the notebook on the complete files instead of the bundle, set the same `AII_DEPS_ROOT` before the `io_util` cell runs.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this folder is marked for deletion. `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no entries, because every file here is text under the 10 MB auto-keep floor. Nothing was downloaded, and there is no virtual environment, cache or checkpoint.

The only removed output is `demo_outputs/`, the notebook's run folder. Running the notebook recreates it in about 25 s:

```bash
jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```
````

### [211] ASSISTANT · 2026-10-26 11:55:12 UTC

```
I've built `code_demo.ipynb` and `mini_demo_data.json`. The notebook runs end to end in a clean Colab-like environment in 24 s (limit 600 s), with no errors. Its outputs are byte-identical to the published artifact's.

**What the notebook runs.** It runs the record-correction stage of `eval.py` (sections 0a–0h plus the base-model compositions). The code is copied unchanged from `eval.py`, `src/io_util.py` and `src/stats_util.py`; the few notebook changes are marked `# notebook:`. The rest of the original pipeline is shown as reference code but not run. That covers the rival freeze and scoring, the live quote check, the audit and the final assembly; they need inputs that aren't in the bundle, and the quote check needs network access. The original Monte Carlo settings stay in the config cell, since they finish in 11 s.

**Results.**
- The run writes 17 files: `corrections.json`, `corrections_metrics.json`, `ids.json` and 14 tables. All are byte-identical to the published artifact's.
- All 23 headline numbers the notebook prints equal the published values. For example, the primary verdict is BA 0.739, Fisher p 0.235, Spearman 0.537, which makes it INCONCLUSIVE.
- Provenance counts are 186 MATCH, 1 CORRECTED, 15 NEW and 4 CARRIED, as in the record.
- All three TODO 6 checks passed: the exact `GITHUB_DATA_URL` is present, the data file exists, and loading uses the GitHub URL with a local fallback. The URL returned 404 during testing, as expected before the push, so the fallback was used.

**Choices you should know about:**
- **What counts as an "example".** The bundle is one dataset with 53 examples, but each example is one input file the stage reads (cut from 77.7 MB to 3.3 MB), not one data point. Trimming the data rows themselves to 100 would have broken the statistics. I checked that running the original `eval.py` on the bundle and on the full files gives identical outputs.
- **How the code cells were filled.** All markdown and notebook-specific cells were added with NotebookEdit. The 11 cells of original code were filled by a script that copies exact slices of the source, then checked with a diff, so they are guaranteed unchanged.
- **Todo list.** No todo tool was available, so I kept the six TODOs in a scratchpad file; all are done.

The pasted research brief at the end was used only as background for the notebook's introduction.

Files are in the workspace folder:
- `code_demo.ipynb`
- `mini_demo_data.json`
- `make_mini_demo_data.py` (rebuilds the bundle)
- `README.md`
- `.aii/manifest.yaml` (no entries; nothing heavy)
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`
```
